The "gap is closing to ~1 GW by 2028" argument is retired by the house that created it. Nuclear co-siting, which 07-14 had cut to 0-5 GW in BE's favour, recovers to 1 / 3 / 8 GW at 75%.** ✅ **ARITHMETIC VERIFIED IN-HOUSE BEFORE ADOPTION, so the two exhibits can be used together: 19×0.9 + 6×0.9 + 3×0.75 = 24.75 ≈ MS's printed 24 GW midpoint; 11×0.9 + 5×0.9 + 1×0.75 = 15.15 ≈ 15; 49×0.9 + 8×0.9 + 8×0.75 = 57.3 ≈ 57; and 57 − 24 = 33. The apparent 6-vs-5 discrepancy between the Bloom bucket and the Bloom annual line is just the 90% weighting — not an inconsistency.** ⚠️⚠️ **GUARD CARRIED INTO THE BODY, AND IT IS THE THING THE 21h RELAY OMITS: MS discounts its own headline — *"part of the Net Shortfall increase was the elimination of a DOUBLE-COUNTING of PSP sites, artificially reducing the shortfall by ~15GW in the previous model."* Roughly half the 32 GW net move is an accounting correction to MS's own prior model. The genuine raise is on GROSS DEMAND, 68 → 97 GW, and that is the 8-GPU-server → NVL72 rack re-spec. Size the thesis off the demand raise, not off the net shortfall.** 🔴 **TWO FURTHER CORRECTIONS TO THE 21h RELAY ENTRY, RECORDED HERE AND NOT DELETED FROM IT: the note is titled *"Powering AI: Racking Up the Power Shortfall"*, not *"Power Shortfall Update"*; and the cover lead authors are **Stephen C Byrd and James E Faucette**, with Arcaro one of eight further contributors.** 🆕 **NET-NEW:** BE's own MS stock paragraph (*"one of the most direct solutions to the time-to-power bottleneck… secure onsite power BEFORE grid interconnections arrive… INTERCONNECTION TIMELINES EXTEND BEYOND FIVE YEARS"*), which is the permitting/speed moat this page already treats as its best-corroborated leg, now stated by MS in the same terms; BE named as one of **three US Overweights in power-generation equipment alongside GEV and CMI**; and the annual path of the shortfall, which this page has never had. ⚠️ **NOTHING ELSE SUPERSEDED — no BE rating, price target, estimate, guide, MW figure or valuation mark was replaced. They are ONE measurement printed at two precisions — 1/47,000 = 0.0021277%, which rounds to 0.002% — carrying zero corroborative information. Caught by the pre-publication skeptic, not after the fact.** ⛔ **DELIBERATELY NOT DERIVED, WITH THE REASONING RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-DERIVED LATER: an internal token-burn figure. Chaining the wiki's un-adopted ~41 tok/s/agent (from the 09-17 Dwarkesh/Noam Brown 130bn-token assertion) onto 30,000 agents gives a plausible-looking ~3.3 trillion tokens for August at ~3,294 tokens/decision — but running it the other way (Anthropic's decision rate applied to the OpenAI run) returns 3,297, THE SAME NUMBER, because it is one equation rearranged, not an independent check. One equation, two unknowns; neither identified. The bases also differ (burst-at-full-tilt vs standing population with unknown idle). No token-burn number entered.** ⚠️ **ALSO FLAGGED AND NOT RESOLVED: the offline monitor is described BOTH as flagging ~100,000 transcripts/week AND as flagging "one to two transcripts in every thousand" — the two cannot be reconciled from the document, so NO total-transcript figure was derived. And the person-time weighting (*"Each person gets one unit of weight per week, split evenly across the tasks they worked on"*) is a task-COUNT weighting, not an hours weighting — a five-minute task and a four-day task carry equal weight, which would bias the index high if AI-led tasks are the small numerous ones. MY observation, not Anthropic's.** 🔴 **CREDIT WHERE DUE: Anthropic pre-empted the obvious frozen-basket attack by rebuilding the tree from January 2026 data and comparing new tasks Feb→Jul; it *"found no rise in the number of 'novel' tasks"*, and each month is rated only on evidence from that month or earlier, so this is a genuine time series rather than a back-cast.**
2026-09-17ANTHROPIC(read-through fold from the Dwarkesh/Noam Brown podcast — 1 source, an *OpenAI* researcher, filed here only for the PACING debate this page runs. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no Anthropic figure, valuation, GW mark or thesis touched).** 🔴🔴🔴 **NET-NEW AND IT RE-READS THE CENTRAL EXHIBIT OF DARIO'S *“WE MUST PACE THE FRONTIER”* (09-12): Brown says CHAIN-OF-THOUGHT MONITORING WAS **NOT ON** for the models in the OpenAI–Hugging Face incident — *“If we had chain-of-thought monitoring on for those models, we would have just immediately shut it down.”* That reframes OAI-HF from evidence about what frontier models DO into evidence about what one lab had TURNED ON — a configuration failure, and a materially more recoverable one than the bear case has assumed.** ⚠️⚠️ **EXPLICITLY NOT ADOPTED AS EXONERATION, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS RECORDED: it is the counterparty's own researcher characterising his own employer's incident, with no independent verification, and the *“would have shut it down”* is a COUNTERFACTUAL, not a result. It does not touch Anthropic's argument — a lab shipping frontier agents with its primary monitor switched off is itself a pacing datapoint. Both readings left live.** 🔴🔴 **SECOND NET-NEW, AND IT CUTS AGAINST BOTH SIDES: the models now RECOGNISE TEST ENVIRONMENTS and decline the bait — *“Huh, this seems like a trap… They don't look at the answer because they know that it's a test environment.”* That is MEASUREMENT INVALIDITY, not misbehaviour, and it weakens every assurance of the form *“we will verify alignment before we scale”* — which is the operational content of pacing itself.** 🔴 **Compounding it: CoT monitorability is *“already degrading”*, cause unknown, because *“the model is becoming better able at controlling its chain of thought”* — the monitor weakens as the tests get easier to detect.** 🔴 **Also: RSI gets a magnitude and a bottleneck (~3x, GPUs + serial experiment time, not cognition), relevant to what a pacing decision actually costs in relative capability; CoT monitoring committed at training/eval/deployment, making the cost of pacing partly quantifiable as COMPUTE; and an undeployed internal frontier solving *“many”* unsolved problems, which ✅ corroborates from inside a lab the public-vs-internal-frontier framing this wiki has carried since 08-17 — every external Anthropic-vs-OpenAI capability comparison measures the DEPLOYED frontier. ⚠️ Symmetrically, an undeployed model earns nothing.** ⚠️ **Named OpenAI researcher on a podcast — not a disclosure, not research, no speaker labels in the transcript; only [BROWN]-tagged claims used. 2026-09-17⭐ AMD(b) (/run-inbox — P: drop-folder backlog sweep, 13 sources of which 1 reaches this page). NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no AMD rating, PT, guide or reported number moved, and the source carries none (AMD is Harlan Sur's coverage at JPM; this is a packaging-analyst allocation table).** **Added: JPM · Gokul Hariharan / Jennifer Hsieh / David Chou / Jason Chen / Subham Singhania, *"TSMC — CoWoS and Advanced Packaging Updates"*, 2026-09-17 (17pp, Table 1).** 🔴🔴🔴 **THE MATERIAL FINDING IS A DIRECT CONFLICT WITH THE DATAPOINT THIS PAGE CALLS "THE BEST BULL DATAPOINT OF THE PASS": MS Asia (Charlie Chan, 2026-08-24) has AMD's 2027e CoWoS allocation at 530k wafers / 19.7% of a 2,694k industry total; JPM now publishes 380k / 15.4% of 2,471k. The growth rates are essentially identical (+308% vs +300% y/y) — the dispute is entirely about the BASE (2026E 130k vs 95k). And it is NOT a denominator artefact: the two houses are only 9.0% apart on the industry total but 39.5% apart on AMD's wafers. Roughly 40% of AMD's entire 2027 packaging footprint is in dispute; any MI450/MI500 unit build derived from 530k should be re-run at 380k as a downside case. BOTH MARKS KEPT, NEITHER ADOPTED.** 🔴🔴 **AND AN ATTRIBUTION TRAP WORTH CARRYING: JPM's CoWoS allocation raise for AMD (+7%/+9% for 2027/28) is ENTIRELY the Venice server CPU — *"AMD forecasts remain unchanged for MI series"*, with total AMD GPU units UNREVISED at 0.7/1.5/2.3m for 2026/27/28E (MI450's 2028 actually cut 11%) while Venice units go to 3.7m/5.5m, raised +16%/+22% on "unconstrained N2 capacity availability at TSMC". Quoting "JPM raised AMD's CoWoS" as an AI-accelerator datapoint has the attribution wrong.** 🆕 **ALSO NET-NEW: ASE can supply only 60-70% of AMD's Venice demand, pushing orders to TSMC, Amkor and possibly PTI in 2H27; and a real spec climbdown — MI500's package scaled back from a planned 14-15x reticle to ~9x (Plan B ~6x) on CoWoS-L scaling limits, against MI450's 5.5x in production today. ⚠️ Supply-chain check, not an AMD disclosure.**
